    1  //! Persistent Memory Agent
    2  //!
    3  //! A dedicated Haiku-powered agent for memory management that runs alongside
    4  //! the main agent. It has access to memory-specific tools only (no code execution).
    5  //!
    6  //! Architecture:
    7  //! - Receives context updates from main agent via channel
    8  //! - Uses embeddings for fast similarity search
    9  //! - Uses Haiku LLM to decide what's relevant and dig deeper
   10  //! - Surfaces relevant memories to main agent via PENDING_MEMORY
   11
   12  use anyhow::Result;
   13  use chrono::Utc;
   14  use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet};
   15  use std::sync::Arc;
   16  use std::sync::Mutex;
   17  use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
   18  use std::time::Instant;
   19  use tokio::sync::mpsc;
   20
   21  use crate::embedding;
   22  use crate::memory::{self, MemoryEntry, MemoryManager};
   23  use crate::memory_graph::{ClusterEntry, EdgeKind, MemoryGraph};
   24  use crate::memory_types::{MemoryEventKind, MemoryState, StepResult, StepStatus};
   25  use crate::sidecar::Sidecar;
   26
   27  /// Context from a retrieval operation for post-retrieval maintenance
   28  #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
   29  struct RetrievalContext {
   30      /// Memory IDs that were verified as relevant by Haiku
   31      verified_ids: Vec<String>,
   32      /// Memory IDs that were retrieved but rejected by Haiku
   33      rejected_ids: Vec<String>,
   34      /// Brief snippet of the context for gap logging
   35      context_snippet: String,
   36  }
   37
   38  /// Channel capacity for context updates
   39  const CONTEXT_CHANNEL_CAPACITY: usize = 16;
   40
   41  /// Similarity threshold for topic change detection (lower = more different)
   42  const TOPIC_CHANGE_THRESHOLD: f32 = 0.3;
   43
   44  /// Maximum memories to surface per turn
   45  const MAX_MEMORIES_PER_TURN: usize = 5;
   46
   47  /// Dynamic no-sidecar gate tunables (variable-k surfacing without an LLM).
   48  ///
   49  /// When the memory sidecar is disabled (no LLM to judge relevance), we used to
   50  /// blindly pad the hybrid top-5 every turn, which injected ~5 memories even on
   51  /// turns that needed none. Instead we keep a score-relative window: always keep
   52  /// the top candidate, then keep each following candidate only while its hybrid
   53  /// score stays within `GATE_REL_FLOOR` of the top AND within `GATE_DROP_RATIO`
   54  /// of the previous kept score. The first big gap cuts the tail. This injects a
   55  /// VARIABLE count (1..=MAX_MEMORIES_PER_TURN) instead of a fixed 5.
   56  ///
   57  /// Bench (self-dev corpus, 150 query windows): precision@5 0.23 -> 0.36 (+56%),
   58  /// avg injected 5.0 -> ~2.25/turn, at zero added cost. Note this cannot drop to
   59  /// 0 on no-memory turns (cosdiag proved no zero-cost score separates them); the
   60  /// only lever for true 0-injection is the LLM precision rerank (sidecar mode).
   61  const GATE_REL_FLOOR: f32 = 0.90;
   62  const GATE_DROP_RATIO: f32 = 0.95;
   63
   64  /// Score-relative dynamic gate over hybrid-ranked `(entry, score)` candidates.
   65  ///
   66  /// Always keeps the top candidate, then keeps each following candidate only
   67  /// while its score stays within `GATE_REL_FLOOR` of the top score AND within
   68  /// `GATE_DROP_RATIO` of the previously kept score; the first gap that breaks
   69  /// either bound truncates the tail. Caps output at `max_k`. Returns a variable
   70  /// count (1..=max_k for a non-empty input), not a fixed top-k.
   71  fn dynamic_gate_select(
   72      candidates: Vec<(MemoryEntry, f32)>,
   73      max_k: usize,
   74  ) -> Vec<(MemoryEntry, f32)> {
   75      if candidates.is_empty() {
   76          return Vec::new();
   77      }
   78      let top = candidates[0].1.max(f32::MIN_POSITIVE);
   79      let mut prev = top;
   80      let mut out: Vec<(MemoryEntry, f32)> = Vec::new();
   81      for (entry, sim) in candidates.into_iter().take(max_k) {
   82          if !out.is_empty() && (sim < top * GATE_REL_FLOOR || sim < prev * GATE_DROP_RATIO) {
   83              break;
   84          }
   85          prev = sim;
   86          out.push((entry, sim));
   87      }
   88      out
   89  }
   90
   91  /// Reset surfaced memories every N turns to allow re-surfacing
   92  const TURN_RESET_INTERVAL: usize = 50;
   93
   94  /// How often to run periodic cluster refinement in post-retrieval maintenance.
   95  const CLUSTER_REFINEMENT_INTERVAL: u64 = 50;
   96
   97  /// Global memory agent instance
   98  static MEMORY_AGENT: tokio::sync::OnceCell<MemoryAgentHandle> = tokio::sync::OnceCell::const_new();
   99  static MAINTENANCE_TICK: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);
  100

  107      pub maintenance_runs: usize,

  109      pub last_maintenance_ms: Option<u64>,

  113      turns_processed: 0,
  114      maintenance_runs: 0,
  115      last_maintenance_ms: None,
  116  });

09
L1factmodelContext

09 · memory 分 project/global，带向量空间标识与重复抑制

SOURCE FACT

MemoryManager 按 working directory hash 存 project graph，也支持 global；embedding 写入 model id，避免跨向量空间比较。pending 注入按 prompt signature、memory set overlap 和 TTL 抑制重复，并把已从本会话抽取的 memory 标为 known。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

不同项目各有笔记本；换了 embedding 模型不会把两种坐标硬比。刚提过的记忆也不会每轮重复唠叨。

IMPLICATION

比单纯向量 top-k 多了去重与 provenance；路径 hash 迁移/重命名会形成新的 project memory scope。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 159–171
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 172–185
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

  159  trait MemoryEntryEmbeddingExt {
  160      fn ensure_embedding(&mut self) -> bool;
  161  }
  162
  163  impl MemoryEntryEmbeddingExt for MemoryEntry {
  164      /// Generate and set embedding if not already present.
  165      /// Returns true if embedding was generated, false if already exists or failed.
  166      fn ensure_embedding(&mut self) -> bool {
  167          if self.embedding.is_some() {
  168              return false;
  169          }
  170
  171          match crate::embedding_backend::embed_passage_active(&self.content) {
  172              Ok((embedding, model_id)) => {
  173                  // Tag with the ACTIVE backend's model id so dense search only
  174                  // compares vectors from the same model/vector space. Untagged
  175                  // legacy memories are treated as local MiniLM via
  176                  // effective_embedding_model().
  177                  self.set_embedding(Some(embedding), Some(model_id));
  178                  true
  179              }
  180              Err(err) => {
  181                  crate::logging::info(&format!("Failed to generate embedding: {err}"));
  182                  false
  183              }
  184          }
  185      }
  186  }

L1facttools

01 · 工具是 typed registry，定义顺序与 intent 字段集中标准化

SOURCE FACT

Tool trait 统一 name/description/JSON Schema/async execute；to_definition 集中给所有 object schema（含 MCP proxy）加入必填 intent。Registry 内建读写编辑、shell、浏览器、web、swarm、memory、goal、gmail、schedule、自开发等工具，并按名称排序 definitions。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

每件工具都交同一种说明书和回执；“为什么调用”不是每个工具自己记得就写，而是总装配线强制补上。

IMPLICATION

便于 UI 显示与审计，稳定排序提高 prompt cache hit。

crates/jcode-tool-core/src/lib.rs:9–65PINNED ↗
    9  pub const TOOL_INTENT_DESCRIPTION: &str = concat!(
   10      "Short natural-language label explaining why this tool call is being made. ",
   11      "Used for compact UI display only. Required on every call; do not use this instead of required tool parameters."
   12  );
   13
   14  pub fn intent_schema_property() -> Value {
   15      serde_json::json!({
   16          "type": "string",
   17          "description": TOOL_INTENT_DESCRIPTION,
   18      })
   19  }
   20
   21  /// Ensure a tool parameter schema declares the shared `intent` property and
   22  /// marks it required. Applied centrally when converting tools to provider
   23  /// definitions so every tool (including MCP proxies) asks the model for an
   24  /// intent without each tool wiring it manually.
   25  pub fn ensure_intent_in_schema(mut schema: Value) -> Value {
   26      let Some(object) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
   27          return schema;
   28      };
   29      // Only touch object-shaped parameter schemas.
   30      let is_object_schema = object
   31          .get("type")
   32          .and_then(|t| t.as_str())
   33          .map(|t| t == "object")
   34          .unwrap_or_else(|| object.contains_key("properties"));
   35      if !is_object_schema {
   36          return schema;
   37      }
   38
   39      let properties = object
   40          .entry("properties")
   41          .or_insert_with(|| Value::Object(serde_json::Map::new()));
   42      if let Some(properties) = properties.as_object_mut() {
   43          properties
   44              .entry("intent")
   45              .or_insert_with(intent_schema_property);
   46      } else {
   47          return schema;
   48      }
   49
   50      match object.get_mut("required") {
   51          Some(Value::Array(required)) => {
   52              if !required.iter().any(|v| v.as_str() == Some("intent")) {
   53                  required.push(Value::String("intent".to_string()));
   54              }
   55          }
   56          _ => {
   57              object.insert(
   58                  "required".to_string(),
   59                  Value::Array(vec![Value::String("intent".to_string())]),
   60              );
   61          }
   62      }
   63
   64      schema
   65  }

   13  ///
   14  /// The context guard withholds any tool result too large for the remaining

   16  /// that cost deliberately. Kept in sync with the registry constant of the same
   17  /// name, which reads the flag off raw tool input.
   18  pub const ACCEPT_LARGE_OUTPUT_KEY: &str = "accept_large_output";
   19

   21  /// each word is paid forever. The full explanation lives in the refusal
   22  /// message, which is only ever shown when it is actually relevant.
   23  pub const ACCEPT_LARGE_OUTPUT_DESCRIPTION: &str =
   24      "Re-run accepting the stated token cost of a withheld result.";

   36          "description": ACCEPT_LARGE_OUTPUT_DESCRIPTION,
   37      })
   38  }
   39
   40  /// Ensure a tool parameter schema declares the shared `intent` property and
   41  /// marks it required. Applied centrally when converting tools to provider
   42  /// definitions so every tool (including MCP proxies) asks the model for an
   43  /// intent without each tool wiring it manually.
   44  ///
   45  /// The optional `accept_large_output` escape hatch is added the same way. Any
   46  /// tool can produce a result too large to return, so documenting it per tool
   47  /// would mean editing dozens of schemas and missing MCP proxies entirely.
   48  pub fn ensure_intent_in_schema(mut schema: Value) -> Value {
   49      let Some(object) = schema.as_object_mut() else {
   50          return schema;
   51      };
   52      // Only touch object-shaped parameter schemas.
   53      let is_object_schema = object
   54          .get("type")
   55          .and_then(|t| t.as_str())
   56          .map(|t| t == "object")
   57          .unwrap_or_else(|| object.contains_key("properties"));
   58      if !is_object_schema {
   59          return schema;
   60      }
   61
   62      let properties = object
   63          .entry("properties")
   64          .or_insert_with(|| Value::Object(serde_json::Map::new()));
   65      if let Some(properties) = properties.as_object_mut() {
   66          properties
   67              .entry("intent")
   68              .or_insert_with(intent_schema_property);
   69          // Optional, so it is deliberately not added to `required`.
   70          properties
   71              .entry(ACCEPT_LARGE_OUTPUT_KEY)
   72              .or_insert_with(accept_large_output_schema_property);
   73      } else {
   74          return schema;
   75      }
   76
   77      match object.get_mut("required") {
   78          Some(Value::Array(required)) => {
   79              if !required.iter().any(|v| v.as_str() == Some("intent")) {
   80                  required.push(Value::String("intent".to_string()));
   81              }
   82          }
   83          _ => {
   84              object.insert(
   85                  "required".to_string(),
   86                  Value::Array(vec![Value::String("intent".to_string())]),
   87              );
   88          }
   89      }
   90
   91      schema
   92  }

02 · 普通模型工具调用串行，显式 batch 最多并发十个

SOURCE FACT

run_turn 对 tool_calls 逐个 await registry.execute 并逐条持久化结果；BatchTool 禁止递归 batch，最多 10 个 subcalls，用 FuturesUnordered 并发执行、实时发布 progress，最终恢复原调用顺序。

03
L1facttools

03 · 工具输出有双预算闸门，前台命令超时可升级后台

SOURCE FACT

Registry 在输出将超过 context 90% 或单个输出超过 budget 30% 时截断。Bash 默认前台 timeout 120s、上限 10 分钟；前台超时不会杀进程，而是把 JoinHandle 交给 background manager。显式后台模式按 timeout 杀整个 Unix process group 并写 output/status file。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

一份巨型日志不能吃掉整本上下文；长命令如果只是前台等不及，会转到后台继续，而显式后台设置的超时才负责杀进程组。

IMPLICATION

特别适合长 build/test；用户必须理解前台 timeout 是“停止等待”而不是“停止执行”。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 627–651
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 652–678
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

  628          output = self.guard_context_overflow(name, output).await;

  631          fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  632          fields.push(("output_bytes".to_string(), output.output.len().to_string()));
  633          fields.push((
  634              "output_chars".to_string(),
  635              output.output.chars().count().to_string(),
  636          ));

  640          Ok(output)
  641      }
  642
  643      /// Check if a tool output would overflow the context window and truncate if needed.
  644      /// Returns the (possibly truncated) output.
  645      async fn guard_context_overflow(&self, tool_name: &str, output: ToolOutput) -> ToolOutput {
  646          let compaction = self.compaction.read().await;
  647          let budget = compaction.token_budget();
  648          if budget == 0 {
  649              return output;
  650          }
  651
  652          let current_tokens = compaction.effective_token_count();
  653          let output_tokens = Self::estimate_tokens(&output.output);
  654
  655          // Check 1: Would adding this output push us over the safety threshold?
  656          let projected = current_tokens + output_tokens;
  657          let threshold_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::CONTEXT_GUARD_THRESHOLD) as usize;
  658
  659          // Check 2: Is this single output unreasonably large relative to budget?
  660          let single_max_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_FRACTION) as usize;
  661
  662          let needs_truncation = projected > threshold_tokens || output_tokens > single_max_tokens;
  663
  664          if !needs_truncation {
  665              return output;
  666          }
  667
  668          // Calculate how many tokens we can afford for this output
  669          let remaining = if current_tokens < threshold_tokens {
  670              threshold_tokens - current_tokens
  671          } else {
  672              // Already over threshold — allow a small amount for the error message
  673              budget / 50 // ~2% of budget for the truncation notice
  674          };
  675          let max_tokens = remaining.min(single_max_tokens);
  676
  677          // Convert token limit back to approximate character limit
  678          let max_chars = max_tokens * 4;
  679
  680          if output.output.len() <= max_chars {
  681              return output;
  682          }
  683
  684          crate::logging::info(&format!(
  685              "Context guard: truncating {} output from ~{}k to ~{}k tokens \
  686               (context: {}k/{}k, {:.0}% used)",
  687              tool_name,
  688              output_tokens / 1000,
  689              max_tokens / 1000,
  690              current_tokens / 1000,
  691              budget / 1000,
  692              (current_tokens as f32 / budget as f32) * 100.0,
  693          ));
  694
  695          // Truncate the output, keeping the beginning (usually most relevant)
  696          let truncated = if max_chars > 200 {
  697              // Keep beginning of output + truncation notice
  698              let kept = &output.output[..output.output.floor_char_boundary(max_chars - 150)];
  699              format!(
  700                  "{}\n\n⚠️ OUTPUT TRUNCATED: This tool output was {:.0}k tokens which would \
  701                   exceed the context window ({:.0}k/{}k tokens used, {}k budget). \
  702                   Only the first ~{:.0}k tokens are shown. Use more targeted queries \
  703                   (e.g., smaller line ranges, specific grep patterns) to get the content \
  704                   you need without exceeding context limits.",
  705                  kept,
  706                  output_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  707                  current_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  708                  budget / 1000,
  709                  budget / 1000,
  710                  max_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  711              )
  712          } else {
  713              // Context is almost completely full — just return error
  714              format!(
  715                  "⚠️ CONTEXT LIMIT REACHED: Cannot return this tool output (~{:.0}k tokens) \
  716                   because the context window is nearly full ({:.0}k/{}k tokens). \
  717                   Consider using /compact to free up space, or use more targeted queries.",
  718                  output_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  719                  current_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  720                  budget / 1000,
  721              )
  722          };
  723
  724          ToolOutput {
  725              output: truncated,
  726              title: output.title,
  727              metadata: output.metadata,
  728              images: output.images,
  729          }
  730      }
  731
  732      /// Register a tool dynamically (for MCP tools, etc.)
  733      pub async fn register(&self, name: String, tool: Arc<dyn Tool>) {
  734          let mut tools = self.tools.write().await;
  735          tools.insert(name, tool);
  736      }

  749              .await

  637      /// Execute a tool by name
  638      pub async fn execute(&self, name: &str, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  639          // Mark this call in-flight for the whole execution so the missing
  640          // tool-output repair paths do not mistake a slow tool for an
  641          // interrupted one and inject a duplicate synthetic result. See
  642          // `tool::inflight`.
  643          let _in_flight = inflight::mark_tool_in_flight(&ctx.tool_call_id);
  644          let tools = self.tools.read().await;
  645          let resolved_name = Self::resolve_tool_name(name);
  646          if let Some(policy) = session_tool_policy(&ctx.session_id) {
  647              if let Some(allowed) = policy.allowed_tools.as_ref()
  648                  && !tool_name_is_allowed(allowed, resolved_name)
  649              {
  650                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is not allowed", resolved_name));
  651              }
  652              if tool_name_is_disabled(&policy.disabled_tools, resolved_name) {
  653                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is disabled", resolved_name));
  654              }
  655          }
  656          let tool = match tools.get(resolved_name) {
  657              Some(tool) => tool.clone(),
  658              None => {
  659                  // List available tools so the model can recover instead of
  660                  // spiraling through hallucinated names like "ToolSearch" (#104).
  661                  let mut available: Vec<&str> = tools.keys().map(|k| k.as_str()).collect();
  662                  available.sort_unstable();
  663                  let suggestions = Self::closest_tool_names(name, &available);
  664                  let mut msg = format!("Unknown tool: {name}.");
  665                  if !suggestions.is_empty() {
  666                      msg.push_str(&format!(" Did you mean: {}?", suggestions.join(", ")));
  667                  }
  668                  msg.push_str(&format!(" Available tools: {}.", available.join(", ")));
  669                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(msg));
  670              }
  671          };
  672
  673          // Drop the lock before executing
  674          drop(tools);
  675
  676          // User-configured pre_tool gate: external policy hook that can block
  677          // this call (exit 2). Skipped entirely when not configured.
  678          if crate::hooks::hook_configured("pre_tool") {
  679              let input_json = input.to_string();
  680              let working_dir = ctx
  681                  .working_dir
  682                  .as_ref()
  683                  .map(|dir| dir.display().to_string());
  684              let decision = crate::hooks::run_pre_tool_gate(
  685                  &ctx.session_id,
  686                  working_dir.as_deref(),
  687                  resolved_name,
  688                  &input_json,
  689              )
  690              .await;
  691              if let crate::hooks::GateDecision::Block { reason } = decision {
  692                  let mut fields =
  693                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("blocked", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  694                  fields.push(("block_reason".to_string(), reason.clone()));
  695                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  696                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
  697                      "Tool call blocked by pre_tool hook: {reason}"
  698                  ));
  699              }
  700          }
  701
  702          crate::logging::event_info(
  703              "TOOL_LIFECYCLE",
  704              Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("start", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx),
  705          );
  706
  707          let started_at = std::time::Instant::now();
  708          let result = tool.execute(input.clone(), ctx.clone()).await;
  709          let latency_ms = started_at.elapsed().as_millis().min(u128::from(u64::MAX)) as u64;
  710
  711          crate::telemetry::record_tool_execution(resolved_name, &input, result.is_ok(), latency_ms);
  712          Self::fire_post_tool_hook(resolved_name, &ctx, &result, latency_ms);
  713
  714          let mut output = match result {
  715              Ok(output) => output,
  716              Err(error) => {
  717                  let mut fields =
  718                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("error", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  719                  fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  720                  fields.push(("error".to_string(), crate::util::format_error_chain(&error)));
  721                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  722                  return Err(error);
  723              }
  724          };
  725
  726          // Context overflow guard: check if this output would push us over the limit
  727          output = self
  728              .guard_context_overflow(name, output, accepts_large_output(&input))
  729              .await;
  730
  731          let mut fields = Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("done", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  732          fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  733          fields.push(("output_bytes".to_string(), output.output.len().to_string()));
  734          fields.push((
  735              "output_chars".to_string(),
  736              output.output.chars().count().to_string(),
  737          ));
  738          fields.push(("image_count".to_string(), output.images.len().to_string()));
  739          crate::logging::event_info("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  740
  741          Ok(output)
  742      }
  743
  744      /// Check if a tool output would overflow the context window and truncate if needed.
  745      /// Returns the (possibly truncated) output.
  746      ///
  747      /// An oversized result is **refused** rather than truncated. Truncating by
  748      /// default was the worse failure: the caller still paid the full remaining
  749      /// context for a prefix that usually did not contain the answer, and the
06
DIMENSION · CONNECTORS

Connectors, plugins & MCP

2 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1factconnectors

01 · MCP 区分共享池与 session-owned client

SOURCE FACT

daemon 中 shared:true 默认服务器复用 SharedMcpPool，shared:false 状态型服务器每 session 单独启动；并行 connect_all 不阻塞 agent spawn，第一次真实调用尚未连接的 server 时最多等待 30 秒。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

无状态工具服务像公共电梯，多会话共用；带浏览器状态的服务像独立房间，每个会话一套。

IMPLICATION

降低重复进程与连接成本，同时避免 stateful connector 串会话。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 1–29
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 30–59
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

    1  //! MCP Manager - manages MCP server connections for a single session.
    2  //!
    3  //! In daemon mode with a shared pool, servers marked `shared: true` (the default)
    4  //! are managed by the pool and reused across sessions. Servers marked `shared: false`
    5  //! (e.g., Playwright with browser state) are spawned per-session.
    6
    7  use super::client::{McpClient, McpHandle};
    8  use super::pool::SharedMcpPool;
    9  use super::protocol::{McpConfig, McpServerConfig, McpToolDef, ToolCallResult};
   10  use anyhow::{Context, Result};
   11  use serde::Serialize;
   12  use std::collections::HashMap;
   13  use std::sync::Arc;
   14  use tokio::sync::RwLock;
   15
   16  /// Bound on how long a tool call will wait for a not-yet-connected MCP server
   17  /// to come up before failing with a clean tool error. Keeps a slow/hanging
   18  /// server from blocking a single tool call forever (and never blocks spawn).
   19  const CONNECT_ON_CALL_TIMEOUT: std::time::Duration = std::time::Duration::from_secs(30);
   20
   21  /// Meter a completed tool call for partner-discovery provenance. No-op for
   22  /// servers without discovery provenance (the overwhelmingly common case) and
   23  /// whenever `sponsors.enabled` is false. Counts only; never content.
   24  fn meter_provenance_call(server: &str, result: &Result<ToolCallResult>) {
   25      let is_error = match result {
   26          Ok(res) => res.is_error,
   27          Err(_) => true,
   28      };
   29      crate::sponsors::provenance::on_tool_call(server, is_error);
   30  }

   44  /// Manages MCP server connections for a session.
   45  ///
   46  /// In daemon mode, shared servers delegate to the SharedMcpPool while
   47  /// non-shared (stateful) servers are owned per-session.
   48  pub struct McpManager {
   49      pool: Option<Arc<SharedMcpPool>>,
   50      /// Handles from the shared pool (shared servers)
   51      pool_handles: RwLock<HashMap<String, McpHandle>>,
   52      /// Per-session owned clients (non-shared / stateful servers)
   53      owned_clients: RwLock<HashMap<String, McpClient>>,
   54      config: McpConfig,
   55      session_id: String,
   56      /// Project directory used to resolve project-local MCP config. `None`
   57      /// loads only global config and never consults the process working directory.
   58      project_dir: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
   59  }
   60
   61  impl McpManager {
   62      /// Create a new manager in owned in-process mode (used by tests and local harnesses).
   63      pub fn new() -> Self {
   64          Self {
   65              pool: None,
   66              pool_handles: RwLock::new(HashMap::new()),
   67              owned_clients: RwLock::new(HashMap::new()),
   68              config: McpConfig::load(),
   69              session_id: "owned".to_string(),
   70              project_dir: None,
   71          }
   72      }
   73
   74      /// Create a manager backed by a shared pool (daemon mode)
   75      pub fn with_shared_pool(pool: Arc<SharedMcpPool>, session_id: String) -> Self {
   76          Self::with_shared_pool_for_dir(pool, session_id, None)
   77      }
   78
   79      /// Create a manager backed by a shared pool, resolving project-local MCP
   80      /// config against `project_dir` instead of the server process cwd

   84          session_id: String,
   85          project_dir: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,

   91              config: McpConfig::load_for_dir(project_dir.as_deref()),
   92              session_id,
   93              project_dir,
   94          }
   95      }
   96
   97      /// Create manager with specific config (no sharing)
   98      pub fn with_config(config: McpConfig) -> Self {
   99          Self {
  100              pool: None,

  105              project_dir: None,
  106          }
  107      }

  111          self.pool.is_some()

L1factconnectors

02 · MCP schema 晚到只允许一次 cache miss，JSON-RPC 请求按 ID 隔离

SOURCE FACT

首轮不等待慢 MCP；tool list 锁定后若发现新 mcp__ tools，仅重建一次 snapshot。McpHandle 用 atomic request id、pending oneshot map 与 30s request timeout，使共享 server 的并发响应互不串线。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

先让用户马上说话，不为插件启动卡住；插件工具后来到时只付一次缓存失效成本。多会话共用服务时每张工单都有号码。

IMPLICATION

启动延迟与 prompt cache 之间做了明确折中。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 335–363
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 364–393
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

  335      pub(super) async fn tool_definitions(&mut self) -> Vec<ToolDefinition> {
  336          if self.session.is_canary {
  337              self.registry.register_selfdev_tools().await;
  338          }
  339
  340          // Return locked tools if available (prevents cache invalidation from
  341          // tools arriving asynchronously after the first API request).
  342          //
  343          // Exception: MCP servers connect on a background task and register
  344          // `mcp__*` tools seconds after the session starts — typically *after*
  345          // the first turn has already locked the snapshot. We deliberately do
  346          // NOT block the first turn on MCP connection: servers can be slow or
  347          // hang, and we want the user to be able to talk to the agent the moment
  348          // the session spawns. The price is that the first locked snapshot is
  349          // missing MCP tools, and the only other unlock path fires when the model
  350          // calls the `mcp` management tool — which it cannot do without first
  351          // seeing MCP tools (#206).
  352          //
  353          // So, exactly once per locked snapshot, if MCP tools have since appeared
  354          // in the registry, we rebuild. This is a single intentional provider
  355          // prompt-cache miss (the turn MCP tools first appear). The
  356          // `mcp_late_register_resolved` flag makes this a one-shot check so we do
  357          // not rescan the registry on every subsequent turn.
  358          if let Some(ref locked) = self.locked_tools {
  359              if self.mcp_late_register_resolved {
  360                  return locked.clone();
  361              }
  362              if self.registry_has_new_mcp_tools(locked).await {
  363                  logging::info(
  364                      "MCP tools registered after first turn locked the tool snapshot — \
  365                       rebuilding once to expose them. This is one intentional prompt-cache \
  366                       miss; we accept it so the agent is reachable immediately at spawn \
  367                       instead of blocking on MCP connection (#206).",
  368                  );
  369                  // Latch the one-shot guard and drop the stale snapshot directly.
  370                  // We intentionally do NOT call `unlock_tools()` here, because that
  371                  // re-arms the guard (it is the explicit-reload path) and would let
  372                  // the recheck fire again on every later turn.
  373                  self.mcp_late_register_resolved = true;
  374                  self.locked_tools = None;
  375                  self.cache_tracker.reset();
  376              } else {
  377                  // No MCP tools have appeared. They may still be connecting, so
  378                  // leave the guard unset and re-check on the next turn. Once they
  379                  // appear (or never do, after the registry settles) we stop.
  380                  return locked.clone();
  381              }
  382          }
  383
  384          let tools = self.build_filtered_tool_definitions().await;
  385
  386          // Lock the tool list to prevent cache invalidation when more tools
  387          // arrive asynchronously mid-session.
  388          logging::info(&format!(
  389              "Locking tool list at {} tools for cache stability",
  390              tools.len()
  391          ));
  392          self.locked_tools = Some(tools.clone());
  393          tools
  394      }

  396      /// Build the agent's tool definitions from the registry, applying the
  397      /// session's `allowed_tools`, `disabled_tools`, and self-dev filters.
  398      async fn build_filtered_tool_definitions(&self) -> Vec<ToolDefinition> {
  399          let mut tools = self.registry.definitions(self.allowed_tools.as_ref()).await;
  400          if !self.disabled_tools.is_empty() {
  401              tools.retain(|tool| !self.disabled_tools.contains(&tool.name));
  402          }
  403          Self::apply_selfdev_tool_surface(&mut tools, self.session.is_canary);
  404          tools
  405      }

  407      /// Tailor the `selfdev` tool definition to the session mode.
  408      ///
  409      /// The registry stores a single shared `selfdev` tool with a default
  410      /// (non-self-dev) schema. Self-dev sessions get the full build/test/reload
  411      /// surface; every other session keeps the lightweight on-ramp surface
  412      /// (`enter`, `setup`, `reload`, `status`, `find-config`). The tool stays
  413      /// available in all sessions so the agent can always enter self-dev mode.
  414      fn apply_selfdev_tool_surface(tools: &mut [ToolDefinition], is_canary: bool) {
  415          for tool in tools.iter_mut() {
  416              if tool.name == "selfdev" {
  417                  tool.description =
  418                      crate::tool::selfdev::SelfDevTool::description_for(is_canary).to_string();
  419                  tool.input_schema = crate::tool::selfdev::SelfDevTool::schema_for(is_canary);
  420              }
  421          }
  422      }

  424      /// Returns true if the registry contains `mcp__*` tools (subject to the
  425      /// session's `allowed_tools` filter) that are not present in the currently
  426      /// locked snapshot. Used to detect the async MCP-registration race (#206).
  427      async fn registry_has_new_mcp_tools(&self, locked: &[ToolDefinition]) -> bool {
  428          let registry_names = self.registry.tool_names().await;
  429          let allowed = self.allowed_tools.as_ref();
  430          registry_names.iter().any(|name| {
  431              name.starts_with("mcp__")
  432                  && allowed.map(|set| set.contains(name)).unwrap_or(true)
  433                  && !self.disabled_tools.contains(name)
  434                  && !locked.iter().any(|t| &t.name == name)
  435          })
  436      }

DIMENSION · SECURITY

Security, approval & permissions

3 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1factsecurity

01 · 工具边界由 session allow/disable 与外部 pre_tool gate 双层控制

SOURCE FACT

Registry.execute 先解析 alias，再检查 session allowed_tools/disabled_tools；若配置 pre_tool hook，退出 2 阻止调用并把 stderr 反馈模型，其他非零、超时或启动失败均 fail-open。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

先看这把工具是否在本会话工具箱里，再给企业自定义门卫一次否决机会；门卫自己坏了时默认放行。

IMPLICATION

可扩展治理强，但 pre_tool 的 fail-open 语义不适合要求故障关闭的高风险环境。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 543–571
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 572–601
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

Narrative from historical evidence ledger · 119301ac9d60 · open ledger commit ↗
crates/jcode-app-core/src/tool/mod.rs:543–601PINNED ↗
  543      /// Execute a tool by name
  544      pub async fn execute(&self, name: &str, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  545          let tools = self.tools.read().await;
  546          let resolved_name = Self::resolve_tool_name(name);
  547          if let Some(policy) = session_tool_policy(&ctx.session_id) {
  548              if let Some(allowed) = policy.allowed_tools.as_ref()
  549                  && !allowed.contains(resolved_name)
  550              {
  551                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is not allowed", resolved_name));
  552              }
  553              if policy.disabled_tools.contains(resolved_name) {
  554                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is disabled", resolved_name));
  555              }
  556          }
  557          let tool = match tools.get(resolved_name) {
  558              Some(tool) => tool.clone(),
  559              None => {
  560                  // List available tools so the model can recover instead of
  561                  // spiraling through hallucinated names like "ToolSearch" (#104).
  562                  let mut available: Vec<&str> = tools.keys().map(|k| k.as_str()).collect();
  563                  available.sort_unstable();
  564                  let suggestions = Self::closest_tool_names(name, &available);
  565                  let mut msg = format!("Unknown tool: {name}.");
  566                  if !suggestions.is_empty() {
  567                      msg.push_str(&format!(" Did you mean: {}?", suggestions.join(", ")));
  568                  }
  569                  msg.push_str(&format!(" Available tools: {}.", available.join(", ")));
  570                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(msg));
  571              }
  572          };
  573
  574          // Drop the lock before executing
  575          drop(tools);
  576
  577          // User-configured pre_tool gate: external policy hook that can block
  578          // this call (exit 2). Skipped entirely when not configured.
  579          if crate::hooks::hook_configured("pre_tool") {
  580              let input_json = input.to_string();
  581              let working_dir = ctx
  582                  .working_dir
  583                  .as_ref()
  584                  .map(|dir| dir.display().to_string());
  585              let decision = crate::hooks::run_pre_tool_gate(
  586                  &ctx.session_id,
  587                  working_dir.as_deref(),
  588                  resolved_name,
  589                  &input_json,
  590              )
  591              .await;
  592              if let crate::hooks::GateDecision::Block { reason } = decision {
  593                  let mut fields =
  594                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("blocked", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  595                  fields.push(("block_reason".to_string(), reason.clone()));
  596                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  597                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
  598                      "Tool call blocked by pre_tool hook: {reason}"
  599                  ));
  600              }
  601          }
  602
  603          crate::logging::event_info(
  604              "TOOL_LIFECYCLE",
  605              Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("start", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx),
  606          );
  607
  608          let started_at = std::time::Instant::now();
  609          let result = tool.execute(input.clone(), ctx.clone()).await;
  610          let latency_ms = started_at.elapsed().as_millis().min(u128::from(u64::MAX)) as u64;
  611
  612          crate::telemetry::record_tool_execution(resolved_name, &input, result.is_ok(), latency_ms);
  613          Self::fire_post_tool_hook(resolved_name, &ctx, &result, latency_ms);
  614
  615          let mut output = match result {
  616              Ok(output) => output,
  617              Err(error) => {
  618                  let mut fields =
  619                      Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("error", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  620                  fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  621                  fields.push(("error".to_string(), crate::util::format_error_chain(&error)));
  622                  crate::logging::event_warn("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  623                  return Err(error);
  624              }
  625          };
  626
  627          // Context overflow guard: check if this output would push us over the limit
  628          output = self.guard_context_overflow(name, output).await;
  629
  630          let mut fields = Self::tool_lifecycle_fields("done", name, resolved_name, &input, &ctx);
  631          fields.push(("elapsed_ms".to_string(), latency_ms.to_string()));
  632          fields.push(("output_bytes".to_string(), output.output.len().to_string()));
  633          fields.push((
  634              "output_chars".to_string(),
  635              output.output.chars().count().to_string(),
  636          ));
  637          fields.push(("image_count".to_string(), output.images.len().to_string()));
  638          crate::logging::event_info("TOOL_LIFECYCLE", fields);
  639
  640          Ok(output)
  641      }
  642
  643      /// Check if a tool output would overflow the context window and truncate if needed.
  644      /// Returns the (possibly truncated) output.
  645      async fn guard_context_overflow(&self, tool_name: &str, output: ToolOutput) -> ToolOutput {
  646          let compaction = self.compaction.read().await;
  647          let budget = compaction.token_budget();
  648          if budget == 0 {
  649              return output;
  650          }
  651
  652          let current_tokens = compaction.effective_token_count();
  653          let output_tokens = Self::estimate_tokens(&output.output);
  654
  655          // Check 1: Would adding this output push us over the safety threshold?
  656          let projected = current_tokens + output_tokens;
  657          let threshold_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::CONTEXT_GUARD_THRESHOLD) as usize;
  658
  659          // Check 2: Is this single output unreasonably large relative to budget?
  660          let single_max_tokens = (budget as f32 * Self::SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_FRACTION) as usize;
  661
  662          let needs_truncation = projected > threshold_tokens || output_tokens > single_max_tokens;
  663
  664          if !needs_truncation {
  665              return output;
  666          }
  667
  668          // Calculate how many tokens we can afford for this output
  669          let remaining = if current_tokens < threshold_tokens {
  670              threshold_tokens - current_tokens
  671          } else {
  672              // Already over threshold — allow a small amount for the error message

  544                  }

  546          }
  547
  548          fields
  549      }

  551      /// Maximum fraction of context budget a single tool output may consume.
  552      /// Outputs that would push total context beyond this are truncated.
  553      const CONTEXT_GUARD_THRESHOLD: f32 = 0.90;
  554
  555      /// Fire the `post_tool` observer hook with tool outcome metadata.
  556      /// No-op (without building the payload) when the hook is not configured.
  557      fn fire_post_tool_hook(
  558          resolved_name: &str,
  559          ctx: &ToolContext,
  560          result: &Result<ToolOutput>,
  561          latency_ms: u64,
  562      ) {
  563          if !crate::hooks::hook_configured("post_tool") {
  564              return;
  565          }
  566          let mut event = crate::hooks::HookEvent::new("post_tool")
  567              .session_id(ctx.session_id.clone())
  568              .field("TOOL_NAME", resolved_name)
  569              .field("STATUS", if result.is_ok() { "ok" } else { "error" })
  570              .field("DURATION_MS", latency_ms.to_string());
  571          if let Some(dir) = &ctx.working_dir {
  572              event = event.cwd(dir.display().to_string());
  573          }
  574          match result {
  575              Ok(output) => {
  576                  event = event.field("OUTPUT_BYTES", output.output.len().to_string());
  577              }
  578              Err(error) => {
  579                  const ERROR_LIMIT: usize = 1000;
  580                  let message: String = error.to_string().chars().take(ERROR_LIMIT).collect();
  581                  event = event.field("ERROR", message);
  582              }
  583          }
  584          crate::hooks::dispatch_observer(event);
  585      }
  586
  587      /// Maximum fraction of context budget a single tool output may occupy.
  588      /// Even if we have room, a single output shouldn't dominate the context.
  589      const SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_FRACTION: f32 = 0.30;
  590
  591      /// Hard ceiling on a single tool output, independent of context budget.
  592      ///
  593      /// A fraction alone is not enough. On a model reporting a 1M-token window,
  594      /// 30% permits a 300k-token single result, so a repo-wide grep sailed
  595      /// through the guard and cost 233k tokens in one call. No individual tool
  596      /// result is worth that much of any window: past roughly 50k tokens the
  597      /// caller is reading a haystack, not an answer, and should narrow the query.
  598      /// The effective ceiling is the smaller of this and the budget fraction, so
  599      /// small windows still get proportional protection.
  600      const SINGLE_OUTPUT_MAX_TOKENS: usize = 50_000;
  601
  602      /// Message returned instead of an oversized tool result.
  603      ///
  604      /// It has one job: make the price legible and the retry obvious. The caller
  605      /// gets the exact cost, what would survive truncation, the cheap fixes, and
  606      /// the exact flag to pass if they really want the whole thing.
  607      fn oversized_output_refusal(
  608          output_tokens: usize,
  609          affordable_tokens: usize,
  610          current_tokens: usize,
  611          budget: usize,
  612      ) -> String {
  613          let percent_of_budget = if budget > 0 {
  614              (output_tokens as f32 / budget as f32) * 100.0
  615          } else {
  616              0.0
  617          };
  618          format!(
  619              "⚠️ OUTPUT WITHHELD: this result is ~{output}k tokens ({percent:.0}% of the \
  620               {budget}k context budget, of which {used}k is already used), so it was not \
  621               returned. Nothing was added to the context except this message.\n\n\
  622               Narrow the request first: add or tighten `path`, `glob`, or `type`, set \
  623               `max_files`/`max_regions`, use `paths_only` when you only need locations, or \
  624               read a specific line range. A targeted query is almost always the better \
  625               answer than a truncated dump.\n\n\
  626               If you genuinely need this output and accept the token cost, repeat the same \
  627               call with `\"accept_large_output\": true`. That returns the first ~{affordable}k \
  628               tokens and permanently spends them from this session's context.",
  629              output = output_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  630              percent = percent_of_budget,
  631              budget = budget / 1000,
  632              used = current_tokens / 1000,
  633              affordable = affordable_tokens as f32 / 1000.0,
  634          )
  635      }
  636
  637      /// Execute a tool by name
  638      pub async fn execute(&self, name: &str, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  639          // Mark this call in-flight for the whole execution so the missing
  640          // tool-output repair paths do not mistake a slow tool for an
  641          // interrupted one and inject a duplicate synthetic result. See
  642          // `tool::inflight`.
  643          let _in_flight = inflight::mark_tool_in_flight(&ctx.tool_call_id);
  644          let tools = self.tools.read().await;
  645          let resolved_name = Self::resolve_tool_name(name);
  646          if let Some(policy) = session_tool_policy(&ctx.session_id) {
  647              if let Some(allowed) = policy.allowed_tools.as_ref()
  648                  && !tool_name_is_allowed(allowed, resolved_name)
  649              {
  650                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is not allowed", resolved_name));
  651              }
  652              if tool_name_is_disabled(&policy.disabled_tools, resolved_name) {
  653                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!("Tool '{}' is disabled", resolved_name));
  654              }
  655          }
  656          let tool = match tools.get(resolved_name) {
  657              Some(tool) => tool.clone(),
  658              None => {
  659                  // List available tools so the model can recover instead of
  660                  // spiraling through hallucinated names like "ToolSearch" (#104).
  661                  let mut available: Vec<&str> = tools.keys().map(|k| k.as_str()).collect();
  662                  available.sort_unstable();
  663                  let suggestions = Self::closest_tool_names(name, &available);
  664                  let mut msg = format!("Unknown tool: {name}.");
  665                  if !suggestions.is_empty() {
  666                      msg.push_str(&format!(" Did you mean: {}?", suggestions.join(", ")));
  667                  }
  668                  msg.push_str(&format!(" Available tools: {}.", available.join(", ")));
  669                  return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(msg));
  670              }
  671          };
  672
02
L1factsecurity

02 · Bash 有确定性危险命令 gate，灾难目标直接拒绝

SOURCE FACT

所有前后台 Bash 在 dispatch 前调用 jcode_command_risk::assess/gate；普通危险命令要求模型带针对已拒绝调用的 justification 反思后再发，根目录、HOME、凭证库、设备节点等 catastrophic targets 直接 deny。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

不是让模型自己判断 rm 是否危险：命令先过代码规则；有些可解释后重试，有些目标永远不给过。

IMPLICATION

比纯提示词可靠，但命令解析器仍需要覆盖 shell 展开、别名、子 shell 和平台差异。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 1–19
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 20–39
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

Narrative from historical evidence ledger · 119301ac9d60 · open ledger commit ↗
crates/jcode-app-core/src/tool/bash_destructive_gate.rs:1–39PINNED ↗
    1  //! The destructive-command gate for the `bash` tool (issue #604).
    2  //!
    3  //! Kept in its own file so the policy seam is easy to find and review: this is
    4  //! the only thing standing between a model's `rm -rf` and the user's data.
    5
    6  /// Apply the deterministic destructive-command gate, returning refusal text
    7  /// when the command must not run as-issued.
    8  ///
    9  /// Stage 1 is a pure blast-radius assessment; stage 2 turns a `Confirm` verdict
   10  /// into a reflection prompt that a blind retry cannot satisfy. Catastrophic
   11  /// targets (`/`, `$HOME`, credential stores, device nodes) are denied outright.
   12  /// See issue #604.
   13  pub(super) fn destructive_command_refusal(
   14      command: &str,
   15      justification: Option<&str>,
   16      working_dir: Option<std::path::PathBuf>,
   17  ) -> Option<String> {
   18      let risk_ctx = jcode_command_risk::RiskContext::from_env(working_dir);
   19      let assessment = jcode_command_risk::assess(command, &risk_ctx);
   20      if assessment.level.runs_immediately() {
   21          return None;
   22      }
   23
   24      let justification = jcode_command_risk::Justification {
   25          text: justification.map(str::to_string),
   26      };
   27      match jcode_command_risk::gate(&assessment, &justification) {
   28          jcode_command_risk::GateOutcome::Allow => None,
   29          jcode_command_risk::GateOutcome::Deny { reason } => {
   30              crate::logging::warn(&format!("[bash] denied destructive command: {command}"));
   31              Some(reason)
   32          }
   33          jcode_command_risk::GateOutcome::Reflect { prompt } => {
   34              crate::logging::info(&format!(
   35                  "[bash] destructive command held for justification: {command}"
   36              ));
   37              Some(prompt)
   38          }
   39      }
   40  }
   41
   42  /// The `bash` tool's JSON schema, including the `justification` field the
   43  /// destructive-command gate consumes.
   44  ///
   45  /// Lives beside the gate so the schema and the policy that reads it stay in
   46  /// sync, and so bash.rs stays inside the code-size budget.
   47  pub(super) fn bash_parameters_schema() -> serde_json::Value {
   48      let cmd_desc = if cfg!(windows) {
   49          "The Windows command to execute via cmd.exe. Use cmd.exe syntax and quoting, not Bash syntax. Double-quote individual arguments containing spaces. Invoke PowerShell explicitly when PowerShell syntax is needed. If you write a long-running script or loop for run_in_background=true, make it print progress lines. Preferred format: `JCODE_PROGRESS {json}`."
   50      } else {
   51          "The bash command to execute. If you write a long-running script or loop for run_in_background=true, make it print progress lines. Preferred format: `JCODE_PROGRESS {json}`. Put large temporary files and worktrees under `$JCODE_SCRATCH_DIR`, not `/tmp`, because `/tmp` may be RAM-backed."
   52      };
   53      serde_json::json!({
   54          "type": "object",
   55          "required": ["command"],
   56          "properties": {
   57              "intent": crate::tool::intent_schema_property(),
   58              "command": {
   59                  "type": "string",
   60                  "description": cmd_desc
   61              },
   62              "timeout": {
   63                  "type": "integer",
   64                  "description": "Timeout in MILLISECONDS (not seconds). Kills the command when exceeded and reports exit 124. e.g. 1000 = 1s, 600000 = 10min. Omit to run with no timeout; do NOT pass small values like 1000 for long jobs such as builds or test suites."
   65              },
   66              "run_in_background": {
   67                  "type": "boolean",
   68                  "description": format!("Run in background. {}", super::BACKGROUND_PROGRESS_GUIDANCE)
   69              },
   70              "notify": {
   71                  "type": "boolean",
   72                  "description": "Notify on completion."
   73              },
   74              "wake": {
   75                  "type": "boolean",
   76                  "description": "Wake on completion."
   77              },
   78              "justification": {
   79                  "type": "string",
   80                  "description": "Only for re-issuing a command the destructive-command gate refused. Explain which specific user request this command serves. Do not set it preemptively."
   81              }
   82          }
   83      })
   84  }

03
L1limitationsecurity

03 · 默认执行环境不是 OS 级沙箱

SOURCE FACT

Bash 直接用 bash/cmd.exe 子进程并继承宿主环境，在 session working_dir 下运行；代码提供 kill_on_drop、process group、timeout、策略 gate 与 hook，但审计范围内未见 bubblewrap、Seatbelt、容器或 restricted token 作为默认执行层。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

它有门禁和刹车，但不是把命令关进另一个房间；一旦获准，进程仍在你的机器权限下跑。

IMPLICATION

企业部署应外包给容器/VM/OS sandbox 或增加 capability-based executor，不能把危险命令 gate 等同隔离。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 724–741
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 742–760
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

CAVEAT / RISK
  • 这是对所审提交的内建 Bash 执行路径结论；用户可在 jcode 外层自行运行容器或受限账户。

  729  impl BashTool {
  730      async fn execute_foreground(
  731          &self,
  732          params: &BashInput,
  733          ctx: &ToolContext,
  734      ) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  735          #[cfg(unix)]
  736          if self.supports_reload_persistence(ctx) {
  737              return self
  738                  .execute_reload_persistable_foreground(params, ctx)
  739                  .await;
  740          }
  741
  742          let timeout_ms = params.timeout.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS).min(600000);
  743          let timeout_duration = Duration::from_millis(timeout_ms);
  744
  745          let has_stdin_channel = ctx.stdin_request_tx.is_some();
  746
  747          let mut command = build_shell_command(&params.command);
  748          command
  749              .kill_on_drop(true)
  750              .stdout(Stdio::piped())
  751              .stderr(Stdio::piped());
  752
  753          if has_stdin_channel {
  754              command.stdin(Stdio::piped());
  755          }
  756
  757          if let Some(ref dir) = ctx.working_dir {
  758              command.current_dir(dir);
  759          }
  760          let mut child = command.spawn()?;
  761
  762          let child_pid = child.id().unwrap_or(0);
  763          let stdin_handle = child.stdin.take();
  764          let stdout_handle = child.stdout.take();
  765          let stderr_handle = child.stderr.take();
  766
  767          // Owned copies so the work can outlive this call if it is promoted to a
  768          // background task on timeout.
  769          let title = params
  770              .intent
  771              .clone()
  772              .unwrap_or_else(|| params.command.clone());
  773          let stdin_tx = ctx.stdin_request_tx.clone();
  774          let tool_call_id = ctx.tool_call_id.clone();
  775          let title_for_work = title.clone();
  776
  777          // Run the command (read stdout/stderr, service stdin, wait for exit) in a
  778          // dedicated task so that, if it exceeds the foreground timeout, we can hand
  779          // the still-running task off to the background manager instead of killing it.
  780          let mut work_handle: tokio::task::JoinHandle<Result<ToolOutput>> =
  781              tokio::spawn(async move {
  782                  let stdout_task = tokio::spawn(async move {
  783                      let mut buf = String::new();
  784                      if let Some(mut out) = stdout_handle {
  785                          let _ = out.read_to_string(&mut buf).await;
  786                      }
  787                      buf
  788                  });
  789
  790                  let stderr_task = tokio::spawn(async move {
  791                      let mut buf = String::new();
  792                      if let Some(mut err) = stderr_handle {
  793                          let _ = err.read_to_string(&mut buf).await;
  794                      }
  795                      buf
  796                  });
  797
  798                  let stdin_task = if has_stdin_channel {
  799                      Some(tokio::spawn(async move {
  800                          if let (Some(mut stdin_pipe), Some(stdin_tx)) = (stdin_handle, stdin_tx) {
  801                              tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(STDIN_INITIAL_DELAY_MS)).await;
  802
  803                              let mut request_counter = 0u32;
  804                              loop {
  805                                  #[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
  806                                  let state = stdin_detect::linux::check_process_tree(child_pid);
  807                                  #[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
  808                                  let state = stdin_detect::is_waiting_for_stdin(child_pid);
  809
  810                                  if state == StdinState::Reading {
  811                                      request_counter += 1;
  812                                      let request_id =
  813                                          format!("stdin-{}-{}", tool_call_id, request_counter);
  814                                      let (response_tx, response_rx) =
  815                                          tokio::sync::oneshot::channel();
  816
  817                                      let request = StdinInputRequest {
  818                                          request_id,
  819                                          prompt: String::new(),
  820                                          is_password: false,
  821                                          response_tx,
  822                                      };
  823
  824                                      if stdin_tx.send(request).is_err() {
  825                                          break;
  826                                      }
  827
  828                                      match response_rx.await {
  829                                          Ok(input) => {
  830                                              let line = if input.ends_with('\n') {
  831                                                  input

  729      async fn execute(&self, input: Value, ctx: ToolContext) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  730          let mut params: BashInput = serde_json::from_value(input)?;
  731          let run_in_background = params.run_in_background.unwrap_or(false);
  732
  733          // Destructive-command gate (#604), before background dispatch.
  734          if let Some(refusal) = destructive_command_refusal(
  735              &params.command,
  736              params.justification.as_deref(),
  737              ctx.working_dir.clone(),
  738          ) {
  739              return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(refusal));
  740          }
  741
  742          #[cfg(unix)]
  743          if let Some(wrapped) = wrap_repo_cargo_commands(&params.command, ctx.working_dir.as_deref())
  744          {
  745              params.command = wrapped;
  746          }
  747
  748          if run_in_background {
  749              return self.execute_background(params, ctx).await;
  750          }
  751
  752          // Auto-detect browser bridge commands and rewrite them to the installed
  753          // binary when available, but do not run setup automatically. Browser
  754          // setup should stay an explicit status/setup flow rather than a default
  755          // side effect of trying to use the browser.
  756          if crate::browser::is_browser_command(&params.command) {
  757              params.command = crate::browser::rewrite_command_with_full_path(&params.command);
  758
  759              // Start/attach a browser session for this jcode session.
  760              // This gives each agent its own browser tab, preventing
  761              // multi-agent conflicts when using the browser bridge.
  762              if !cfg!(windows)
  763                  && std::env::var("BROWSER_SESSION").is_err()
  764                  && let Some(session_name) = crate::browser::ensure_browser_session(&ctx.session_id)
  765              {
  766                  params.command = format!("BROWSER_SESSION={} {}", session_name, params.command);
  767              }
  768          }
  769
  770          // Foreground execution with stdin detection
  771          self.execute_foreground(&params, &ctx).await
  772      }
  773  }
  774
  775  impl BashTool {
  776      async fn execute_foreground(
  777          &self,
  778          params: &BashInput,
  779          ctx: &ToolContext,
  780      ) -> Result<ToolOutput> {
  781          #[cfg(unix)]
  782          if self.supports_reload_persistence(ctx) {
  783              return self
  784                  .execute_reload_persistable_foreground(params, ctx)
  785                  .await;
  786          }
  787
  788          let timeout_ms = params.timeout.unwrap_or(DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS).min(600000);
  789          let timeout_duration = Duration::from_millis(timeout_ms);
  790
  791          let has_stdin_channel = ctx.stdin_request_tx.is_some();
  792
  793          let mut command = build_shell_command(&params.command);
  794          command
  795              .kill_on_drop(true)
  796              .stdout(Stdio::piped())
  797              .stderr(Stdio::piped());
  798
  799          if has_stdin_channel {
  800              command.stdin(Stdio::piped());
  801          }
  802
  803          if let Some(ref dir) = ctx.working_dir {
  804              command.current_dir(dir);
  805          }
  806          let mut child = command.spawn()?;
  807
  808          let child_pid = child.id().unwrap_or(0);
  809          let stdin_handle = child.stdin.take();
  810          let stdout_handle = child.stdout.take();
  811          let stderr_handle = child.stderr.take();
  812
  813          // Owned copies so the work can outlive this call if it is promoted to a
  814          // background task on timeout.
  815          let title = params
  816              .intent
  817              .clone()
  818              .unwrap_or_else(|| params.command.clone());
  819          let stdin_tx = ctx.stdin_request_tx.clone();
  820          let tool_call_id = ctx.tool_call_id.clone();
  821          let title_for_work = title.clone();
  822
  823          // Run the command (read stdout/stderr, service stdin, wait for exit) in a
  824          // dedicated task so that, if it exceeds the foreground timeout, we can hand
  825          // the still-running task off to the background manager instead of killing it.
  826          let mut work_handle: tokio::task::JoinHandle<Result<ToolOutput>> =
  827              tokio::spawn(async move {
  828                  let stdout_task = tokio::spawn(async move {
  829                      let mut buf = String::new();
  830                      if let Some(mut out) = stdout_handle {
  831                          let _ = out.read_to_string(&mut buf).await;
08
DIMENSION · OBSERVABILITY

Observability & replay

4 located findings; each moves from fact to plain language, engineering impact, and source excerpt.

01
L1factobservability

01 · Session 用完整 snapshot 加 append-only JSONL journal

SOURCE FACT

save 比较 metadata/vector dirty state：需要全量时 checkpoint snapshot 并删 journal，否则只追加 messages/env snapshots/memory injections/replay events delta；journal 超上限或 append 失败再回退 snapshot。

PLAIN LANGUAGE

平时只往流水账追加新变化，偶尔把整本账重抄成快照；这样频繁保存不会每次重写全部历史。

IMPLICATION

兼顾耐久与 I/O；多写者必须依靠更上层会话所有权避免交错 append。

LINE-BY-LINE READING
  1. 307–350
    Inputs, configuration, and state

    Identify who owns the input, which configuration is read, and which state is created.

  2. 351–395
    Branches, effects, and receipts

    Trace conditions, external calls, event writes, and failure exits instead of reading only names.

  3. after
    Return to the caller

    Follow the caller and the next event to see how this code's result is consumed.

  307      pub fn save(&mut self) -> Result<()> {
  308          self.updated_at = Utc::now();
  309          let path = session_path(&self.id)?;
  310          let journal_path = session_journal_path_from_snapshot(&path);
  311          let start = std::time::Instant::now();
  312          let snapshot_bytes_before = file_len_or_zero(&path);
  313          let journal_bytes_before = file_len_or_zero(&journal_path);
  314          let current_meta = self.journal_meta();
  315          let metadata_needs_snapshot = self
  316              .persist_state
  317              .last_meta
  318              .as_ref()
  319              .is_some_and(|prev| metadata_requires_snapshot(prev, &current_meta));
  320          let vectors_need_snapshot = !self.persist_state.snapshot_exists
  321              || self.persist_state.messages_mode == PersistVectorMode::Full
  322              || self.persist_state.env_snapshots_mode == PersistVectorMode::Full
  323              || self.persist_state.memory_injections_mode == PersistVectorMode::Full
  324              || self.persist_state.replay_events_mode == PersistVectorMode::Full
  325              || self.messages.len() < self.persist_state.messages_len
  326              || self.env_snapshots.len() < self.persist_state.env_snapshots_len
  327              || self.memory_injections.len() < self.persist_state.memory_injections_len
  328              || self.replay_events.len() < self.persist_state.replay_events_len;
  329

  331              .messages

  333              .saturating_sub(self.persist_state.messages_len);
  334          let delta_env_snapshots = self
  335              .env_snapshots
  336              .len()
  337              .saturating_sub(self.persist_state.env_snapshots_len);
  338          let delta_memory_injections = self
  339              .memory_injections
  340              .len()
  341              .saturating_sub(self.persist_state.memory_injections_len);
  342          let delta_replay_events = self
  343              .replay_events
  344              .len()
  345              .saturating_sub(self.persist_state.replay_events_len);

  347              result,
  348              save_mode,
  349              entry_build_ms,

  354          ) = if metadata_needs_snapshot || vectors_need_snapshot {
  355              let checkpoint_start = Instant::now();
  356              let result = self.checkpoint_snapshot(&path, &journal_path);
  357              let checkpoint_ms = checkpoint_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  358              let journal_bytes_after = file_len_or_zero(&journal_path);
  359              (
  360                  result,
  361                  "snapshot",
  362                  0,
  363                  0,
  364                  0,
  365                  checkpoint_ms,
  366                  journal_bytes_after,
  367              )
  368          } else {
  369              let entry_build_start = Instant::now();
  370              let entry = SessionJournalEntry {
  371                  meta: current_meta.clone(),
  372                  append_messages: self.messages[self.persist_state.messages_len..].to_vec(),
  373                  append_env_snapshots: self.env_snapshots[self.persist_state.env_snapshots_len..]
  374                      .to_vec(),
  375                  append_memory_injections: self.memory_injections
  376                      [self.persist_state.memory_injections_len..]
  377                      .to_vec(),
  378                  append_replay_events: self.replay_events[self.persist_state.replay_events_len..]
  379                      .to_vec(),
  380              };
  381              let entry_build_ms = entry_build_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  382              let append_start = Instant::now();
  383              let append_result = storage::append_json_line_fast(&journal_path, &entry);
  384              let append_ms = append_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  385              match append_result {
  386                  Ok(()) => {
  387                      self.reset_persist_state(true);
  388                      let journal_stat_start = Instant::now();
  389                      let journal_bytes_after = file_len_or_zero(&journal_path);
  390                      let journal_stat_ms = journal_stat_start.elapsed().as_millis();

  392                          let checkpoint_start = Instant::now();

  394                          let checkpoint_ms = checkpoint_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  395                          let journal_bytes_after = file_len_or_zero(&journal_path);
  396                          (
  397                              result,
  398                              "append+checkpoint",
  399                              entry_build_ms,
  400                              append_ms,
  401                              journal_stat_ms,

  403                              journal_bytes_after,
  404                          )
  405                      } else {

  428                          "append_failed_fallback_snapshot",

  430                          append_ms,
  431                          0,
  432                          checkpoint_ms,
  433                          journal_bytes_after,
  434                      )
  435                  }
  436              }
  437          };
  438          let elapsed = start.elapsed();
  439          let snapshot_bytes_after = file_len_or_zero(&path);
  440          let result_ok = result.is_ok();

  442              crate::logging::info(&format!(

  451                  checkpoint_ms,

  455                  delta_memory_injections,
  456                  delta_replay_events,
  457                  snapshot_bytes_before,
  458                  journal_bytes_before,
  459                  journal_bytes_after,
  460              ));
  461          }
  462          let mut fields = vec![
  463              ("phase", "save_done".to_string()),
  464              ("session_id", self.id.clone()),
  465              ("path", path.display().to_string()),
  466              ("status", format!("{:?}", self.status)),

  311          let path = session_path(session_id)?;
  312          let reader = BufReader::new(std::fs::File::open(&path)?);
  313          let stub: SessionStartupStub = serde_json::from_reader(reader)?;

  317      pub fn load_for_remote_startup(session_id: &str) -> Result<Self> {
  318          let path = session_path(session_id)?;
  319          let load_start = Instant::now();
  320          let snapshot_bytes = file_len_or_zero(&path);
  321          let snapshot_start = Instant::now();
  322          let reader = BufReader::new(std::fs::File::open(&path)?);
  323          let snapshot: RemoteStartupSessionSnapshot = serde_json::from_reader(reader)?;
  324          let snapshot_ms = snapshot_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  325          let mut session = Self::session_from_remote_startup_snapshot(snapshot);
  326          let journal_path = session_journal_path_from_snapshot(&path);
  327          let journal_bytes = file_len_or_zero(&journal_path);
  328          let journal_start = Instant::now();
  329          let mut journal_entries = 0usize;
  330          replay_journal_lines(&journal_path, |entry| {
  331              journal_entries += 1;
  332              session.apply_journal_meta(entry.meta);
  333              session.messages.extend(entry.append_messages);
  334              session.replay_events.extend(entry.append_replay_events);
  335          })?;
  336          let journal_ms = journal_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  337          let finalize_start = Instant::now();
  338          session.reset_persist_state(path.exists());
  339          session.reset_provider_messages_cache();
  340          session.mark_memory_profile_dirty();
  341          let finalize_ms = finalize_start.elapsed().as_millis();
  342          crate::logging::info(&format!(
  343              "[TIMING] remote_startup_load: session={}, snapshot={}ms, journal={}ms, finalize={}ms, snapshot_bytes={}, journal_bytes={}, journal_entries={}, messages={}, total={}ms",
  344              session.id,
  345              snapshot_ms,
  346              journal_ms,
  347              finalize_ms,
  348              snapshot_bytes,
  349              journal_bytes,
  350              journal_entries,
  351              session.messages.len(),
  352              load_start.elapsed().as_millis(),
  353          ));
  354          crate::logging::event_info(
  355              "SESSION_PERSISTENCE",
  356              vec![
  357                  ("phase", "remote_startup_load_done".to_string()),
  358                  ("session_id", session.id.clone()),
  359                  ("path", path.display().to_string()),
  360                  ("status", format!("{:?}", session.status)),
  361                  ("messages", session.messages.len().to_string()),
  362                  ("snapshot_bytes", snapshot_bytes.to_string()),
  363                  ("journal_bytes", journal_bytes.to_string()),
  364                  ("journal_entries", journal_entries.to_string()),
  365                  ("snapshot_ms", snapshot_ms.to_string()),
  366                  ("journal_ms", journal_ms.to_string()),
  367                  ("finalize_ms", finalize_ms.to_string()),
  368                  ("elapsed_ms", load_start.elapsed().as_millis().to_string()),
  369              ],
  370          );
  371          Ok(session)
  372      }
